The greatest value our early history and literature has for us is the value of a clue to character, to be returned to again and again in the maze of our infinitely more complicated life and era.

In every nation which has been allowed free development, while it has the qualities common to all humanity, it will be found that some one idea was predominant, and in its predominance regrouped about itself other ideas. With our neighbors I believe the idea of personal liberty has been the inspiring motive of all that is best in its political development, whatever the reactions and oppressions may have been. In ancient Attica the idea of beauty, proportion, or harmony in life so pervaded the minds of the citizens that the surplus revenues of the State were devoted to the beautifying of the city. We find that love for beauty in its art, its literature, its architecture; and to Plato, the highest mind in the Athenian State, Deity itself appeared as Beauty in its very essence. That mighty mid-European State, whose ambitions have upset the world, seems to conceive of the State as power. Other races have had a passion for justice, and have left codes of law which have profoundly affected the life of nations which grew up long after they were dead. The cry of ancient Israel for righteousness rings out above all other passions, and its laws are essentially the laws of a people who desired that morality should prevail. We have to discover for ourselves the ideas which lie at the root of national character, and so inculcate these principles that they will pervade the nation and make it a spiritual solidarity, and unite the best minds in their service, and so control those passionate and turbulent elements which are the cause of the downfall and wreckage of nations by internal dissensions. I desire as much as anyone to preserve our national identity, and to make it worthy of preservation, and this can only be done by the domination of some inspiring ideal which will draw all hearts to it; which may at first have that element of strangeness in it which Ben Jonson said was in all excellent beauty, and which will later become﻿—as all high things we love do finally become﻿—familiar to us, and nearer and closer to us than the beatings of our own hearts.

===Chinese===

任何方针，若不合于民族品格，便不能成功。倘若我在这一点上判断有误，这里写下的文字便属徒然。或许有人要问：谁能从作为爱尔兰历史的这团混沌中，抽绎出一种一再重现的支配性心境或趋势，并断言这些是根本性的？民族品格难以界说，即便在那些历史向全世界敞开的立国久远的国家亦然；而在爱尔兰更是难乎其难——数世纪以来，它未曾依自己的意志、从自己的中心行事，其民族活动主要表现为对外来统治的抗议，或对外部权力的自我调适。我们无法从过去七百年的历史推演出爱尔兰人的政治品格，正如我们无法评定一位我们只在其与窃贼搏斗时才见过的艺术家的天才品质。一个民族的政治品格，唯有当他们在自由中塑造自己的文明时方才显露。要在爱尔兰寻得线索，我们必须绕过那七个世纪的斗争去研究民族的起源，正如词典编纂者为求得一个词的确切含义而追溯其词源。我们的早期历史与文学之于我们的最大价值，便是品格之线索的价值——在我们这远为纷繁的生活与时代的迷宫中，须一次又一次地回返于它。

在每一个被允许自由发展的民族中，尽管它具有全人类共有的品质，我们仍会发现某一个观念曾居于主宰，并在其主宰之下把其他观念重新汇聚于自身周围。在我们的邻邦，我相信，个人自由的观念一直是对其政治发展中一切精华的激励，无论其间有过怎样的反动与压迫。在古代阿提卡，美、匀称或和谐的生活观念如此弥漫于公民心中，以致国家的岁入盈余都被用于城邦的美化。我们在它的艺术、文学与建筑中见到这种对美的爱；而对雅典国家中最高的心灵柏拉图而言，神性本身就显现为本质的美。那个野心搅翻了世界的中欧大国，似乎把国家构想为权力。另一些种族则怀有对正义的热忱，留下了深刻影响诸多民族之生活的法典，而这些民族是在它们死去很久之后才成长起来的。古代以色列对公义的呼喊盖过一切其他激情，它的法律本质上是一个渴望道德得以盛行的民族的法律。我们必须自己去发现那些植根于民族品格底层的观念，并把这些原则如此谆谆灌输，使之弥漫于全民族，使它成为一种精神性的团结，并使最优秀的心灵联合起来为其效力，从而驾驭那些狂热而骚乱的要素——它们正是各民族因内讧而倾覆覆灭的缘由。我同任何人一样渴望保存我们的民族同一性，并使它值得保存；而唯有让某种振奋人心的理想居于主宰、把所有的心都引向它，才能做到这一点。这理想起初或许带有本·琼森（Ben Jonson）所说存在于一切卓越之美中的那种奇异成分，但后来终将——如我们所爱的一切崇高事物最终都会变成的那样——为我们所熟悉，比我们自己心房的搏动更切近、更亲近我们。

===Original===

When ideals which really lie at the root of our being are first proclaimed, all that is external in life protests. So were many great reformers martyred, but they left their ideals behind them in the air, and men breathed them and they became part of their very being. Nationality is a state of consciousness, a mood of definite character in our intellectual being, and it is not perceived first except in profound meditation; it does not become apparent from superficial activities any more than we could, by looking at the world and the tragic history of mankind, discover that the Kingdom of Heaven is within us. That knowledge comes to those who go within themselves, and not to those who seek without for the way, the truth, and the life. But, once proclaimed, the incorruptible spiritual element in man intuitively recognizes it as truth, and it has a profound effect on human action. There is, I believe, a powerful Irish character which has begun to reassert itself in modern times, and this character is in essentials what it was two thousand years ago. We discover its first manifestation in the ancient clans. The clan was at once aristocratic and democratic. It was aristocratic in leadership and democratic in its economic basis. The most powerful character was elected as chief, while the land was the property of the clan. That social order indicates the true political character of the Irish. Races which last for thousands of years do not change in essentials. They change in circumstance. They may grow better or worse, but throughout their history the same fundamentals appear and reassert themselves. We can see later in Irish literature or politics, as powerful personalities emerged and expressed themselves, how the ancient character persisted. Swift, Goldsmith, Berkeley, O’Grady, Shaw, Wilde, Parnell, Davitt, Plunkett, and many others, however they differed from each other, in so far as they betrayed a political character, were intensely democratic in economic theory, adding that to an aristocratic freedom of thought. That peculiar character, I believe, still persists among our people in the mass, and it is by adopting a policy which will enable it to manifest once more that we will create an Irish civilization, which will fit our character as the glove fits the hand. During the last quarter of a century of comparatively peaceful life the cooperative principle has once more laid hold on the imagination of the Irish townsman and the Irish countryman. The communal character is still preserved. It still wills to express itself in its external aspects in a communal civilization, in an economic brotherhood. That movement alone provides in Ireland for the aristocratic and democratic elements in Irish character. It brings into prominence the aristocracy of character and intelligence which it is really the Irish nature to love, and its economic basis is democratic. A large part of our failure to achieve anything memorable in Ireland is due to the fact that, influenced by the example of our great neighbors, we reversed the natural position of the aristocratic and democratic elements in the national being. Instead of being democratic in our economic life, with the aristocracy of character and intelligence to lead us, we became meanly individualistic in our economics and meanly democratic in leadership. That is, we allowed individualism﻿—the devilish doctrine of every man for himself﻿—to be the keynote of our economic life; where, above all things, the general good and not the enrichment of the individual should be considered. For our leaders we chose energetic, commonplace types, and made them represent us in the legislature; though it is in leadership above all that we need, not the aristocracy of birth, but the aristocracy of character, intellect, and will. We had not that aristocracy to lead us. We chose instead persons whose ideas were in no respect nobler than the average to be our guides, or rather to be guided by us. Yet when the aristocratic character appeared, however imperfect, how it was adored! Ireland gave to Parnell﻿—an aristocratic character﻿—the love which springs from the deeps of its being, a love which it gave to none other in our time.

===Chinese===

当那些真正植根于我们存在之根底的理想初次被宣告时，生活中一切外在的东西都起而抗议。许多伟大的改革者正是这样招致殉难的；但他们把理想留在了身后的空气里，人们呼吸着它们，它们便成了人们存在本身的一部分。民族性是一种意识状态，是我们智识存在中一种品格确定的心境；它唯有在深彻的沉思中才初次被察觉，绝不会从表浅的活动中显明，正如我们不可能靠观望这个世界与人类的悲剧历史，便发现天国（the Kingdom of Heaven）就在我们之内。那种知识只降临于返求诸己的人，而不降临于向外寻求道路、真理与生命的人。而一旦宣告出来，人身上那不可败坏的精神要素便直觉地认出它是真理，它便对人类行动发生深远的影响。我相信，存在一种强有力的爱尔兰品格，它已开始在现代重申自身，而它在本质上仍是两千年前的那副品格。我们在古代的诸氏族中发现它最初的显现。氏族既是贵族的，又是民主的：它在领导权上是贵族的，在经济基础上是民主的。最强有力的人物被推选为首领，而土地则是氏族的财产。那种社会秩序标示着爱尔兰人真正的政治品格。绵延数千年的种族在本质上并不改变，改变的是境遇。它们或许变好，或许变坏，但在其整个历史中，同样的根本之物一再出现、一再重申自身。稍后在爱尔兰的文学或政治中，随着强有力的个性崭露头角、表达自身，我们可以看到那种古老的品格如何持续存在。斯威夫特（Swift）、哥尔德斯密斯（Goldsmith）、贝克莱（Berkeley）、奥格雷迪（O’Grady）、萧伯纳（Shaw）、王尔德（Wilde）、帕内尔（Parnell）、戴维特（Davitt）、普伦基特（Plunkett），以及许多其他人，无论彼此如何不同，就其显露出政治品格的方面而言，他们在经济学说上都强烈地倾向民主，并把这一点加诸一种贵族式的思想自由。我相信，那种独特的品格仍普遍存留于我们人民之中；而唯有采取一种能让它再度显现的方针，我们才能创造出一种爱尔兰文明——它契合我们的品格，如手套之契合于手。在这相对太平的四分之一个世纪里，合作原则再度攫住了爱尔兰城里人与乡下人的想象。共同体的品格仍被保存着。它仍愿在自己外在的方面把自己表达为一种共同体的文明、一种经济上的手足之情。在爱尔兰，唯有这场运动同时安顿了爱尔兰品格中的贵族要素与民主要素。它使品格与才智之贵族脱颖而出——爱戴这种贵族，实在是爱尔兰的天性——而它的经济基础是民主的。我们在爱尔兰未能成就任何值得铭记之事，很大程度上缘于：受我们伟大邻邦榜样的影响，我们把国家存在中贵族要素与民主要素的天然位置颠倒过来了。本应在经济生活中行民主、受品格与才智之贵族引领的我们，却变得在经济上卑劣地个人主义，在领导上卑劣地民主。也就是说，我们容许个人主义——那种「人人为己」的魔鬼学说——成为我们经济生活的主调；而在经济生活中，最应考虑的应是公共的善，而非个人的发财致富。我们选来做领袖的是些精力充沛而平庸的类型，让他们在立法机构中代表我们；尽管在领导一事上，我们最需要的不是出身之贵族，而是品格、才智与意志之贵族。我们没有那种贵族来引领我们。我们选的倒是些见识毫不比常人高明的人来做我们的向导——或者不如说，来做受我们引导的人。然而，每当贵族式的品格出现时，无论它多么不完美，它受到何等的崇敬！爱尔兰给予帕内尔——一个贵族式的品格——那种发自其存在深处的爱，一种它在我们这个时代不曾给予任何别人的爱。

===Original===

With our great neighbors what are our national characteristics were reversed. They are an individualistic race. This individualism has expressed itself in history and society in a thousand ways. Being individualistic in economics, they were naturally democratic in politics. They have a genius for choosing forcible average men as leaders. They mistrust genius in high places, Intensely individualistic themselves, they feared the aristocratic character in politics. They desired rather that general principles should be asserted to encircle and keep safe their own national eccentricity. They have gradually infected us with something of their ways, and as they were not truly our ways we never made a success of them. It is best for us to fall back on what is natural with us, what is innate in character, what was visible among us in the earliest times, and what, I still believe, persists among us﻿—a respect for the aristocratic intellect, for freedom of thought, ideals, poetry, and imagination, as the qualities to be looked for in leaders, and a bias for democracy in our economic life. We were more Irish truly in the heroic ages. We would not then have taken, as we do today, the huckster or the publican and make them our representative men, and allow them to corrupt the national soul. Did not the whole vulgar mob of our politicians lately unite to declare to the world that Irish nationality was impossible except it was floated on a sea of liquor? The image of Kathleen ni Houlihan anciently was beauty in the hearts of poets and dreamers. We often thought her unwise, but never did we find her ignoble; never was she without a flame of idealism in her eyes, until this ignoble crew declared alcohol to be the only possible basis of Irish nationality.

In the remote past we find the national instincts of our people fully manifested. We find in this early literature a love for the truth-teller and for the hero. Indeed they did not choose as chieftains of their clans men whom the bards could not sing. They reverenced wisdom, whether in king, bard, or ollav, and at the same time there was a communal basis for economic life. This heroic literature is, as our Standish O’Grady declared, rather prophecy than history. It reveals what the highest spirits deemed the highest, and what was said lay so close to the heart of the race that it is still remembered and read. That literature discloses the character of the national being, still to be manifested in a civilization, and it must flame out before the tale which began among the gods is closed. Whatever brings this communal character into our social order, and at the same time desires the independent aristocratic intellect, is in accord with the national tradition. The cooperative movement is the modern expression of that mood. It is already making a conquest of the Irish mind, and in its application to life predisposing our people to respect for the man of special attainments, independent character, and intellect. A social order which has made its economics democratic in character needs such men above all things. It needs aristocratic thinkers to save the social order from stagnation, the disease which eats into all harmonious life. We shall succeed or fail in Ireland as we succeed or fail to make democracy prevail in our economic life, and aristocratic ideals to prevail in our political and intellectual life.

===Chinese===

与我们伟大的邻邦相比，我们的民族特性恰恰是颠倒的。他们是一个个人主义的种族。这种个人主义在历史与社会中以千百种方式表达自己。经济上是个人主义的，他们在政治上便自然是民主的。他们有一种天才，专会挑选强悍的平庸之辈作领袖。他们猜忌身居高位的天才；他们自己便极度个人主义，因而惧怕政治中的贵族式品格。他们毋宁希望确立种种普遍原则，用来环护他们自己的民族怪癖并保其无虞。他们已渐渐把他们的一些做派传染给了我们；而这些做派并非真正属于我们，我们从未把它们做成功过。我们最好回返于对我们而言是自然的东西、品格中天生固有的东西、最早年代就在我们中间可见的东西，以及——我仍然相信——至今仍存留于我们中间的东西：对贵族式才智的敬重，对思想自由、理想、诗歌与想象的敬重，把它们视为领袖身上应觅得的品质，同时在经济生活中偏向民主。在英雄时代，我们其实更是爱尔兰人。那时，我们绝不会像今天这样，把叫卖小贩或酒馆老板捧作我们的代表人物，任由他们腐蚀国民的灵魂。我们政客中那一整群粗鄙的乌合之众，不是最近还联合起来向世界宣告：爱尔兰民族除非漂浮在酒的海洋之上，否则便不可能存在吗？卡瑟琳·尼·胡利汉（Kathleen ni Houlihan）的形象，古时在诗人与梦想者的心中本是美。我们常以为她不明智，却从不觉得她卑劣；她的眼中从不缺一簇理想主义的火焰——直到这伙卑劣之徒宣称，酒精是爱尔兰民族唯一可能的基础。

在遥远的过去，我们发现我们人民的民族本能得到充分的显现。我们在这早期文学中看到对言真者与对英雄的爱。的确，凡吟游诗人无法歌颂的人，他们不会选作氏族的首领。他们崇敬智慧，无论它见于王者、吟游诗人还是奥拉夫（ollav）身上；与此同时，经济生活有着共同体的基础。正如我们的斯坦迪什·奥格雷迪（Standish O’Grady）所言，这英雄文学与其说是历史，不如说是预言。它揭示最高的心灵视何者为最高；其所言说的一切与这个种族的心贴得如此之近，以致至今仍被记诵、仍被阅读。那文学披露了国家存在的品格——它尚待在一个文明中显现出来；在那始于诸神的故事落幕之前，它必得熊熊燃起。凡能把这共同体品格带进我们的社会秩序、同时又渴求独立的贵族式才智的东西，都与民族传统相合。合作运动便是那种心境的现代表达。它已在征服爱尔兰的心灵，并在其施于生活的过程中，使人民预先倾向于敬重那些具有专门造诣、独立品格与才智之士。一种使自身经济具有民主性质的社会秩序，最需要的便是这样的人。它需要贵族式的思想家来拯救社会秩序，使其免于停滞——那是侵蚀一切和谐生活的疾病。我们在爱尔兰将成功抑或失败，就看我们能否使民主盛行于我们的经济生活，使贵族式的理想盛行于我们的政治与智识生活。

===Original===

In all things it is best for a people to obey the law of their own being. The lion can never become the ox, and “one law for the lion and the ox is oppression.”

Now that the hammer of Thor is wrecking our civilizations, is destroying the body of European nationalities, the spirit is freer to reshape the world nearer to the heart’s desire. Necessity will drive us along with the rest to recast our social order and to fix our ideals. Necessity and our own hearts should lead us to a brotherhood in industry. It should be horrible to us the thought of the greedy profiteer, the pursuit of wealth for oneself rather than the union of forces for the good of all and the creation of a brotherly society. The efforts of individuals to amass for themselves great personal wealth should be regarded as ignoble by society, and as contrary to the national spirit, as it is indeed contrary to all divine teaching. Our ideal should be economic harmony and intellectual diversity. We should regard as alien to the national spirit all who would make us think in flocks, and discipline us to an unintellectual commonalty of belief. The life of the soul is a personal adventure, a quest for the way and the truth and the life. It may be we shall find the ancient ways to be the true ways, but if we are led to the truth blindfolded and without personal effort, we are like those whom the Scripture condemns for entering into Paradise, not by the straight gate, but over the wall, like thieves and robbers. If we seek it for ourselves and come to it, we shall be true initiates and masters in the guild.

No people seem to have greater natural intelligence than the Irish. No people have been so unfortunately cursed with organizations which led them to abnegate personal thought, and Ireland is an intellectual desert where people read nothing and think nothing; where not fifty in a hundred thousand could discern the quality of thought in the *Politics* of Aristotle or the *Republic* of Plato as being in any way deeper than a leading article in one of their daily papers. And we, whose external life is so mean, whose ignorance of literature is so great, are yet flattered by the suggestion that we have treasures of spiritual and intellectual life which should not be debased by external influences, and so it comes about that good literature is a thing unpurchasable except in some half-dozen of the larger towns. Any system which would suppress the aristocratic, fearless, independent intellect should be regarded as contrary to the Irish genius and inimical to the national being.

===Chinese===

在一切事情上，一个民族最好服从其自身存在的法则。狮子永远变不成牛，而「予狮子与牛以同一法律，即是压迫」。

如今，托尔（Thor）之锤正在砸毁我们的文明，正在摧毁欧洲诸民族的躯体，精神于是得以更自由地把世界重塑得更接近心底的愿望。必然性将驱使我们同其余各方一道重铸我们的社会秩序、确立我们的理想。必然性与我们自己的心，应当把我们引向产业中的手足之情。一想到贪婪的牟利之徒，想到为一己追逐财富——而不是为公共的善联合众力、缔造一个手足之邦——我们便应感到可怖。个人为一己积聚巨量私财的努力，应当被社会视为卑劣，视为违背民族精神——正如它确实违背一切神圣的教诲。我们的理想应当是：经济上的和谐，智识上的多样。凡要使我们成群结伙地思考、把我们驯服于一种非智识的信仰齐一的人，我们都应视为民族精神的异己。灵魂的生活是一场个人的历险，是对道路、真理与生命的求索。或许我们终将发现古道才是真道；但倘若我们被蒙着眼、不经个人的努力而被领到真理面前，我们便像那些受圣经谴责的人——他们进入乐园，不是经由窄门，而是像贼与强盗那样翻墙而入。倘若我们亲自寻求它而抵达它，我们便将成为行会中真正的入门者与师傅。

似乎没有哪个民族的天赋智识比爱尔兰人更高，也没有哪个民族如此不幸地被种种组织所诅咒——这些组织引他们放弃个人的思考。爱尔兰是一片智识的荒漠，那里的人什么都不读、什么都不想；在那里，十万人中不足五十人能分辨出亚里士多德《政治学》（Politics）或柏拉图《理想国》（Republic）中思想的品质，究竟比他们某一份日报的社论深在哪里。而我们——外部生活如此卑陋、对文学的无知如此之深的我们——竟还因这样的说法而沾沾自喜：我们拥有精神与智识生活的宝藏，不应被外部影响所贬损；于是结果便是，除了在寥寥五六座较大的城镇，好文学竟是一件买不到的东西。任何要压制那贵族式的、无所畏惧的、独立的才智的制度，都应被视为违背爱尔兰的民族天才、有害于国家存在。

## XVI / 十六

===Original===
Among the many ways men have sought to create a national consciousness, a fountain of pride to the individual citizen, is to build a strong body for the great soul, and it would be an error to overlook﻿—among other modern uprisings of ancient Irish character﻿—the revival of the military spirit and its possible development in relation to the national being. National solidarity may be brought about by pressure from without, or by the fusion of the diverse elements in a nation by a heat engendered from within. But to Create national solidarity by war is to attain but a temporary and unreal unity, a gain like theirs who climb into the Kingdom not by the straight gate, but over the wall like a robber. When one nation is threatened by another, great national sacrifices will be made, and the latent solidarity of its humanity be kindled. But when the war is over, when the circumstances uniting the people for a time are past, that spirit rapidly dies, and people begin their old antagonisms because the social order, in its normal working, does not constantly promote a consciousness of identity of interest.

Almost all the great European states have fortified their national being by militarism. Everything almost in their development has been subordinated to the necessities of national defense, and hence it is only in times of war there is any real manifestation of national spirit. It is only then that the citizens of the Iron Age feel a transitory brotherhood. It is a paradoxical phenomenon, possible only in the Iron Age, that the highest instances of national sacrifice are evoked by warfare﻿—the most barbarous of human enterprises. To make normal that spirit of unity which is now only manifested in abnormal moments in history should be our aim; and as it is the Iron Age, and material forces are more powerful than spiritual, we must consider how these fierce energies can be put in relation with the national being with least debasement of that being. If the body of the national soul is too martial in character, it will by reflex action communicate its character to the spirit, and make it harsh and domineering, and unite against it in hatred all other nations. We have seen that in Europe but yesterday. The predominance in the body of militarist practice will finally drive out from the soul those unfathomable spiritual elements which are the body’s last source power in conflict, and it will in the end defeat its own object, which is power. When nations at war call up their reserves of humanity to the last man capable of bearing arms, their leaders begin also to summon up those bodiless moods and national sentiments which are the souls of races, and their last and most profound sources of inspiration and deathless courage. The war then becomes a conflict of civilizations and of spiritual ideals, the aspirations and memories which constitute the fundamental basis of those civilizations. Without the inspiration of great memories or of great hopes, men are incapable of great sacrifices. They are rationalists, and the preservation of the life they know grows to be a desire greater than the immortality of the spiritual life of their race. A famous Japanese general once said it was the power to hold out for the last desperate quarter of an hour which won victories, and it is there spiritual stamina reinforces physical power. It is a mood akin to the ecstasy of the martyr through his burning. Though in these mad moments neither spiritual nor material is consciously differentiated, the spiritual is there in a fiery fusion with all other forces. If it is absent, the body unsupported may take to its heels or will yield. It has played its only card, and has not eternity to fling upon the table in a last gamble for victory.
===Chinese===
在人们力求创造民族意识（national consciousness）——它是个体公民引以为豪的泉源——的诸多途径之中，有一条，便是为伟大的灵魂建造一具强健的躯体；而在古代爱尔兰品格的诸般现代勃发之中，倘若对尚武精神（the military spirit）的复兴、以及它在与国家存在的关系上可能有的发展视而不见，那将是一个错误。民族团结（national solidarity）可以由外部压力造成，也可以由民族内部自生的热力，把民族中各异的成分熔铸为一体而造成。但借战争创造民族团结，所达致的不过是一种短暂而不真的统一；这所得，恰如那些不肯由正门、却像强盗一样翻墙爬进天国之人的所得。当一个民族受到另一个民族的威胁时，巨大的民族牺牲将会做出，其人性中潜藏的团结也会被点燃。但战事一过，那曾使人民一时联合起来的境况既成既往，这种精神便迅速死去，人们重新开始旧日的对立，因为社会秩序在其正常的运转中，并不持续培育对利益的同一的意识。

欧洲几乎所有大国，都曾借军国主义（militarism）加固它们的国家存在。它们发展中的几乎一切，都从属于国防的必需；因此，唯有在战时，民族精神才有真正的显现。也唯有在那时，黑铁时代的公民们才感到一种转瞬即逝的手足之情。这是一个唯有黑铁时代才可能有的悖谬现象：民族牺牲的最高典范，竟由战争——人类一切事业中最野蛮的那桩——所唤起。使那种如今只在历史上反常时刻方才显现的统一精神成为常态，应当是我们的目标；而既然这是黑铁时代，物质的力量强于精神的力量，我们就必须思量：这些凶猛的能量，怎样才能与国家存在发生关联，而使这一存在蒙受的贬损最小。倘若国民灵魂的躯体在品格上过于尚武，它便会经由反作用把这一品格传导给精神，使精神变得严酷而专横，并使天下所有别的民族在仇恨中联合起来与它为敌。这样的事，昨天我们才在欧洲见过。躯体中军事实践的独占，最终会把那些深不可测的精神元素从灵魂中驱除出去——它们本是躯体在冲突中最后的力量泉源——于是它终将挫败自己的目标，而那目标正是权力。当交战中的各民族把人性中的后备力量一直征召到最后一个能够执戈的人，它们的领袖们也开始召起那些无形的心绪与民族情感——它们是各个种族的灵魂，是灵感与不死勇气的最后、最深的泉源。于是战争变成了诸文明与诸精神理想的冲突，变成了构成那些文明根本基础的种种抱负与记忆的冲突。没有伟大记忆或伟大希望的启迪，人们便做不出伟大的牺牲。他们成了理性主义者，保全他们所熟识的生活，会长成一个比其种族精神生活之不朽更大的欲望。一位日本名将曾经说过：赢得胜利的，是熬过最后那绝望的一刻钟的力量；而正是在那里，精神的坚韧补强了身体的力量。那是一种近乎殉道者在烈焰焚身之狂喜中的心境。在这些疯狂的时刻，精神与物质虽不曾被有意识地分辨，精神却在那里，与一切别的力量作着火热的熔合。它若缺席，失去支撑的躯体便可能撒腿而逃，或者干脆屈服。它已打出了手中唯一的牌，再没有一张永恒可以掷上桌面，作争胜的最后一掷。

===Original===
A military organization may strengthen the national being, but if it dominates it, it will impoverish its life. How little Sparta has given to the world compared with Attica. Yet when national ideals have been created they assume an immeasurably greater dignity when the citizens organize themselves for the defense of their ideals, and are prepared to yield up life itself as a sacrifice if by this the national being may be preserved. A creed always gains respect through its martyrs. We may grant all this, yet be doubtful whether a militarist organization should be the main support of the national being in Ireland. The character of the ideal should, I believe, be otherwise created, and I am not certain that it could not be as well preserved and defended by a civil organization, such as I have indicated, as by armed power. Our geographical position and the slender population of our country also make it evident that the utmost force Ireland could organize would make but a feeble barrier against assault by any of the greater States. We have seen how Belgium, a country with a population larger than that of Ireland, was thrust aside, crushed and bleeding, by one stroke from the paw of its mighty neighbor.1 The military and political institutions of a small country are comparatively easy to displace, but it would be a task infinitely more difficult to destroy ideals or to extinguish a national being based on a social order, democratic and cooperative in character, the soul of the country being continually fed by institutions which, by their very nature, would be almost impossible to alter unless destruction of the whole humanity of the country was aimed at. National ideals, based on a cooperative social order, would have the same power of resistance almost as a religion, which is, of all things, most unconquerable by physical force, and, when it is itself militant, the most powerful ally of military power. The aim of all nations is to preserve their immortality. I do not oppose the creation of a national army for this purpose. There are occasions when the manhood of a nation must be prepared to yield life rather than submit to oppression, when it must perish in self-contempt or resist by force what wrong would be imposed by force. But I would like to point out that for a country in the position of Ireland the surest means of preserving the national being by the sacrifice and devotion of the people are economic and spiritual.
===Chinese===
军事组织可以强国国家存在，然而它若反过来支配了国家存在，便会使它的生命陷于贫瘠。斯巴达（Sparta）给予这个世界的，比起阿提卡来何其之少！然而，民族理想一经创造，当公民们为捍卫这些理想而把自己组织起来、并准备献出生命本身作为牺牲——倘若国家存在可由此得以保全——这些理想便获得一种无可计量的、远为巨大的尊严。一种信条，总是经由它的殉道者赢得敬意。这一切我们尽可承认，却仍不免怀疑：军事组织是否应当成为爱尔兰国家存在的主要支柱。我相信，理想的品格应当以别的方式铸成；而且我不敢断言，由一种民间组织（civil organization）——如我曾指明的那种——来保存它、捍卫它，就一定不及武力行之有效。我们的地理位置，连同我国人口的单薄，也使这一点显而易见：爱尔兰所能组织的最大力量，在任何一个大国的进攻面前，只不过是一道脆弱的屏障。我们已经看到，比利时（Belgium）——一个人口比爱尔兰还稠密的国家——如何被它那强大邻邦的一爪挥击，掀在一旁，碾碎，淌着血。1 小国的军事建制与政治建制，相对容易搬倒；然而要摧毁种种理想，或要扑灭一种建立在具有民主与合作品格的社会秩序之上的国家存在，却是一桩无比艰难得多的任务——这个国家的灵魂，正被种种建制不断喂养着，而这些建制依其本性几乎无从更改，除非所图者正是毁灭这个国家的全部人性。建立在合作社会秩序之上的民族理想，将几乎具有与宗教同样的抵抗力；而宗教，在一切事物中最不可为武力所征服，一旦它自身奋起而斗，又是武力最强有力的盟友。一切民族的目标，都是保全自身的不朽。为此目的而创建一支国民军（national army），我并不反对。总有一些时刻，一个民族的男儿必须准备好献出生命而不肯屈服于压迫——那时，他们要么在自轻自鄙中灭亡，要么以武力抵抗那本要以武力强加的不义。但我愿意指出：对于处境如爱尔兰的国家而言，借人民的牺牲与忠忱来保全国家存在，最可靠的手段是经济上的与精神上的。

===Original===
Our political life in the past has been sordid and unstable because we were uncultured as a nation. National ideals have been the possession of the few in Ireland, and have not been diffused. That is the cause of our comparative failure as a nation. If we would create an Irish culture, and spread it widely among our people, we would have the same unfathomable sources of inspiration and sacrifice to draw upon in our acts as a nation as the individual has who believes he is immortal, and that his life here is but a temporary foray into time out of eternity.

Yet we have much to learn from the study of military organization. The great problem of all civilizations is the creation of citizens: that is, of people who are dominated by the ideal of the general welfare, who will sink private desire and work harmoniously with their fellow-citizens for the highest good of their race. While we may all agree that war brings about an eruption of the arcane and elemental forces which lie normally in the pit of human life, as the forces which cause earthquakes lie normally asleep in the womb of the world, none the less we must admit that military genius has discovered and applied with mastery a law of life which is of the highest importance to civilization﻿—far more important to civil even than to military development﻿—and that is the means by which the individual will forget his personal danger and sacrifice life itself for the general welfare. In no other organization will men in great masses so entirely forget themselves as men will in battle under military discipline. What is the cause of this? Can we discover how it is done and apply the law to civil life?
===Chinese===
我们过去的政治生活之所以卑污而动荡，是因为我们作为一个民族缺乏教养。民族理想在爱尔兰一直是少数人的私产，从未广播于众。这便是我们作为民族相对失败的原因。倘若我们能创造出一种爱尔兰文化，并把它广泛播撒于人民之中，那么我们作为民族，在行动中便同样有深不可测的灵感与牺牲的泉源可供汲取，一如那个人所拥有的——他相信自己不朽，相信他在此世的一生，不过是从永恒中暂时突入时间的一场奔袭。

然而，对于军事组织的研究，我们大有可学。一切文明的重大问题，是公民的造就：也就是说，造就这样一些人——他们受公共福祉（the general welfare）之理想的支配，肯淹没私人的欲望，与同胞和谐共事，为其种族的最高之善而劳作。我们尽可以一致承认：战争造成了秘奥而基本的力量的喷发，这些力量平日蛰伏在人类生活的渊底，正如造成地震的力量平日沉睡在大地的子宫之中；尽管如此，我们必须承认，军事天才已经发现并娴熟地运用了一条生命法则，这条法则对文明极端重要——对民间的发展甚至比对军事的发展更为重要——那就是使个人忘却一己之危、为公共福祉牺牲生命本身的手段。在任何别的组织里，成千上万的人们都不会像军纪之下的战场上那样，把自我忘得如此彻底。这是什么缘故？我们能否发现这是如何做到的，并把这条法则应用于民间生活？

===Original===
The military discipline works miracles. The problem before the captains of armies is to take the body of man, the most naturally egoistic of all things, which hates pain and which will normally take to its legs in danger and try to save itself, and to dominate it so that the body and the soul inhabiting it will stand still and face all it loathes. And the problem is solved in the vast majority of cases. After military training the civilians who formerly would fly before a few policemen will manfully and heroically stand, not the blows of a baton, but a whole hail of bullets, a cannonade lasting through a day; nay, they will for weeks and months, day by day, risk and lose life for a cause, for an idea, at a word of command. They may not have half as good a cause to lose life for as they had as a mob of angry civilians, but they will face death now, and the chances of mutilation and agony worse than death. Can we inspire civilians with the same passionate self-forgetfulness in the pursuit of the higher ideals of peace? Men in a regiment have to a large extent the personal interests abolished. The organization they now belong to supports them and becomes their life. By their union with it a new being is created. Exercise, drill, maneuver, accentuate that unity, and esprit de corps arises, so that they feel their highest life is the corporate one; and that feeling is fostered continually, until at last all the units, by some law of the soul, are as it were in spite of themselves, in spite of the legs which want to run, in spite of the body which trembles with fear, constrained to move in obedience to the purpose of the whole organism expressed by its controlling will; and so we get these devoted masses of men who advance again and again under a hail more terrible than Dante imagined falling in his vision of the fiery world.
===Chinese===
军事纪律创造着奇迹。摆在军队统帅们面前的问题是：把人的身体——一切事物之中最天然利己的那样东西，它憎恶疼痛，一遇危险照例撒腿便逃、只图自保——拿过来加以统御，使这身体连同寓居其中的灵魂站定不动，直面它所憎恶的一切。而这问题，在绝大多数情形下竟告解决。经过军事训练之后，那些从前见几个警察就要逃散的平民，会刚勇而英雄地站住，承受的不再只是警棍的击打，而是整片弹雨，是持续一整日的炮轰；不止如此，他们会一连数周、数月，日复一日，为一项事业、为一个理念，仅凭一声号令，便去赴险、去丧生。他们今日为之舍生的事业，兴许还不及他们当初作为一伙愤怒平民所怀抱的一半正当；但他们如今直面死亡，直面伤残的种种可能，直面比死亡更甚的苦痛。我们能否以同样的炽烈忘我，去鼓舞平民追求和平的更高理想？一个团里的士兵，其个人利益在很大程度上已被废除。他们如今所属的组织供养着他们，成了他们的生命。借由与它的结合，一个新的存在被创造了出来。演习、操练、机动，使那统一愈发彰显，团队精神（esprit de corps）油然而生，于是他们感到，自己最高的生命便是那集体的生命；这情感又被不断地培育，直到最后，所有的单元，凭灵魂的某条法则，仿佛全然身不由己——不顾那想要奔逃的双腿，不顾那因恐惧而颤抖的身体——被迫遵照整个机体的目的、即其掌控意志所表达的目的而行动；于是我们得到了这些赤诚献身的人的群体：他们在一片比但丁（Dante）在其火界异象中所想象坠落的雹击更为可怕的弹雨之下，一次又一次地向前推进。

===Original===
There is nothing like it in civilian life, but yet the aim of the higher minds in all civilizations is to create a similar devotion to civic ideals, so that men will not only, as Pericles said, “give their bodies for the commonwealth,” but will devote mind, will, and imagination with equal assiduity and self-surrender to the creation of a civilization which will be the inheritance of all and a cause of pride to everyone, and which will bring to the individual a greater beauty and richness of life than he could finally reach by the utmost private efforts of which he was capable.

I believe that an organization of society, such as I have indicated, would evolve gradually a similar passion for the general zeal, having, without the stern restraint militarism imposes on its units, a like power of turning the thoughts to the general good.
===Chinese===
平民生活中无可与之相比者，然而一切文明中较高尚的心灵，其目标正是要创造出一种对公民理想（civic ideals）的类似献身，使人们不仅如伯里克利所说的那样「把身体献给共同体」，而且以同样的勤勉与自我奉献，把心智、意志与想象投注于一种文明的创造——这文明将是所有人共同的遗产，是每个人自豪的因由，而它将带给个人的，是一种比他凭自身所能尽的最大私人努力终可达到的更美好、更丰饶的生活。

我相信，一种如我曾指明的那样的社会组织，将会逐渐演化出一种同样的献身于公共热忱的激情；它无须军国主义加诸其成员的那种严酷约束，却具有把思想转向公共的善的同等力量。

===Original===
I may say also that to create a militarist organization, before the natural principles to be safeguarded are well understood and a common possession of all the people in the country, would be a danger akin to the peril of allowing children to play with firearms. We may find it a bad business to create natural ideals as they are required, just as it is a perilous business to try to create an army when a country is in a state of war. If we do not rapidly create a national culture embodying the fundamental ideas we wish to see prevailing in society our volunteer armies will be subject to influences from the baser sort of politicians who would force party aims on the country. We shall have a wretched future unless the soul of the country can dominate the physical forces in it, unless ideals of national conduct, liberty of speech and thought, of justice and brotherhood, exist to inspire and guide it, and are recognized by all and appealed to by all parties equally.

We are standing on the threshold of nationhood, and it is problems like these we should be setting ourselves to solve, unless we are to be an unimportant province of the world, a mere administrative area inhabited by a quite undistinguished people.
===Chinese===
我还要说：在有待捍卫的种种自然原则尚未被充分理解、尚未成为全国人民的共同财富之前，便去创建一个军事组织，其危险近于听任孩童玩弄枪械。临到需要时才去制造自然理想，我们也许会发现这是桩糟糕的营生，正如一个国家身处战争状态时才试图创建一支军队，是桩危险的营生一样。倘若我们不迅速创造出一种民族文化（national culture），把那些我们愿见其盛行于社会的根本观念体现其中，我们的志愿军（volunteer armies）便会受制于卑劣政客的影响——这些人一心要把党派的目标强加于国家。除非这个国家的灵魂能够主宰它体内的种种物质力量，除非民族行为操守的理想、言论与思想的自由、正义与手足之情的理想，存在于此以启迪它、引导它，并为所有人所承认、为各党派平等地援引，否则我们的未来必将是悲惨的。

我们正站在立国（nationhood）的门槛上；除非我们甘愿做世界上一个无足轻重的行省，做一个不过由毫不出众的人民栖居的行政区，否则，我们应当立意去求解的，正是这样一些问题。

## XVII / 十七

===Original===
But there are other methods of devotion to the national being possible to us through collective action, and I was moved to imagine one, having once received a letter from a bloodthirsty correspondent﻿—one of that rather numerous class whose minds are always loaded with ball cartridge, whose fingers are always on the trigger, and who are always calling on the authorities not to hesitate to shoot. He wrote to me during a railway strike, advocating military conscription in order that railway men who went out on strike could be called up by the military authorities, as the French railway strikers were, and who were subject to martial law if they disobeyed. I do not think with those who believe the venerable remedy of bloodletting is the best cure for social maladies; and I would have thought no more about that stern disciplinarian, but my mind went playing about the idea of conscription, and there came to me some thoughts which I wish to put on record in the hope that our people in some future, when the social order will create public spirit and the passion for the State more plentifully than it does today, may recur to the idea and apply it. Nearly every State in the world demands from youth a couple of years’ service in the army. There they are trained to defend their country﻿—even, if necessary, to slay their own countrymen. There is much that is abhorrent to the imagination in the idea of war, and I am altogether with that noble body of men who are trying, by means of arbitration treaties, to solve national differences by reason rather than by force. But we all recognize something noble in the spirit of the nation where the community agrees that every man shall give up some years of his life to the State for the preservation of the State, and may be called upon to surrender life absolutely in that service. While the manhood of a race does this on the whole with cheerfulness, there must be something of high character in the manhood of that nation. A certain gravity attaches to national decisions which are made, as it were, upon the slopes of death, because none are exempt from service, and there is no delirious mob ready to yell for a war in which it does not run the risk of having its own dirty skin perforated by bullets. In Ireland we have never had military conscription, for reasons which are well known to all, and upon which I need not enter. I am well satisfied it should be so, for it leaves open to us the possibility of a much nobler service, one which has never yet been attempted by any modern nation, and that is civil conscription.

I throw out this suggestion, which may hold the imagination of those who have noble conceptions of what national life should be and what a nation should work for, in the hope that some time it may fructify. There is a prohibition laid on the people in this island against conscription for military purposes. Is there any reason why we should not have conscription for civil purposes? Why should not every young man in Ireland give up two years of his life in a comradeship of labor with other young men, and be employed under skilled direction in great works of public utility, in the erection of public buildings, the beautifying of our cities, reclamation of waste lands, afforestation, and other desirable objects? The principle of service for the State for military purposes is admitted in every country, even at last by the English-speaking peoples. It is easy to be seen how this principle of conscription could be applied to infinitely nobler ends﻿—to the building up of a beautiful civilization﻿—and might make the country adopting it in less than half a century as beautiful as ancient Attica or majestic as ancient Egypt. While other nations take part of the life of young men for instruction in war, why should not the State in Ireland, more nobly inspired, ask of its young men that they should give equally of their lives to the State, not for the destruction of life, but for the conservation of life? This service might be asked from all﻿—high and low, well and humbly born﻿—except from those who can plead the reasons which exempt people abroad from military service. As things stand today, if the State undertakes any public work, it does it more expensively by far than it would be if undertaken by private enterprise. Every person puts up prices for the State or for municipalities. Labor, land, and materials are all charged at the highest possible rates, whereas if there was any really high conception of citizenship and of the functions of the State, the citizens would agree so that works of public utility, or those which conspired to add to national dignity, should be done at least cost to the community. Where there is no national sacrifice there is no national pride. Because there is no national pride our modern civilizations show meanly compared with the titanic architecture of the cities and majestic civilizations of the past. We know from the ruins of these proud cities that he who walked into ancient Rome, Athens, Thebes, Memphis and Babylon, walked amid grandeurs which must have exalted the spirit. To walk into Manchester, Sheffield, or Liverpool is to feel a weight upon the soul. There is no national feeling for beauty in our industrial civilizations.

Let us suppose Ireland had through industrial conscription about fifty thousand young men every year at its disposal under a national works department. What could be done? First of all it would mean that every young man in the country would have received an industrial training of some kind. The work of technical instruction could be largely carried on in connection with this industrial army. People talk of the benefit of discipline and obedience secured by military service. This and much more could be secured by a labor conscription. Every man in the island would have got into the habit of work at a period of life when it is most necessary, and when too many young men have no serious occupation. Parents should welcome the training and discipline for their children, and certificates of character and intelligence given by the department of national works should open up prospects of rapid employment in the ordinary industrial life of the country when the period of public service was closed. For those engaged there would be a true comradeship in labor, and the phrase, “the dignity of labor,” about which so much cant has been written, would have a real significance where young men were working together for the public benefit with the knowledge that any completed work would add to the health, beauty, dignity, and prosperity of the State. In return for this labor the State should feed and clothe its industrial army, educate them, and familiarize them with some branch of employment, and make them more competent after this period of service was over to engage in private enterprise. Two years of such training would dissipate all the slackness, lack of precision, and laziness which are so often apparent in young men who have never had any strict discipline in their homes, and whom parental weakness has rendered unfit for the hard business of life.

===Chinese===
但通过集体行动，我们还可以有别的方式献身于国家存在；我之所以动了念头去构想其中一种，是因为曾收到一位嗜血的通信者的来信——此君属于一个人数颇为众多的阶级：他们的脑子里永远上着实弹，手指永远搭在扳机上，并且总在呼吁当局开枪切勿迟疑。他在一场铁路罢工期间给我来信，主张实行征兵制（military conscription），好让参加罢工的铁路员工能被军事当局征召入伍——一如法国铁路罢工者的遭遇——他们若抗命不遵，便要受军法制裁（martial law）。我不与那些人同道——他们相信，放血这种古老而备受敬重的疗法，是医治社会痼疾的最佳方子；对那位严苛的纪律主义者，我本也不会再多想，只是我的心智围着征役这个观念盘桓嬉游了一番，于是有一些想法来到我心头。我想把它们记录在案，希望我们的人民在将来某个时候——当社会秩序比今日更充沛地造就公共精神与对国家的热忱之时——或许会重新回到这个观念上来，并将其付诸实行。世上几乎每一个国家都要求青年为陆军贡献几年服役。在那里，他们受训保卫自己的国家——必要时，甚至受训杀死自己的同胞。战争的观念里，有许多令想象深恶痛绝的东西；而我全然与那群高贵的人士站在一起——他们正试图借仲裁条约（arbitration treaties），以理性而非武力解决民族之间的纷争。但是，当一个民族的共同体约定：人人皆须为保全国家而向国家献出生命中的几年，且可能被要求在那项服役中把生命整个交出去——对这样一个民族的精神，我们都承认其中有某种高贵的东西。当一个种族的男儿整体上甘愿如此，这个民族的男儿气概中必有某种高贵的品格。一种非同寻常的庄重，附着于那些仿佛是在死亡的坡道上作出的国家决策，因为无人得免于服役，也没有哪个癫狂的暴民会去叫嚣一场战争——在那场战争里，它自己那身肮脏的皮肉并无被子弹洞穿之虞。在爱尔兰，我们从未实行过征兵制，其原因人尽皆知，我无须置喙。我对事当如此深感满意，因为它给我们留着一种远为高贵的服役的可能——现代国家还从未尝试过的那一种——那就是民役（civil conscription）。

我抛出这个建议——它或许能攫住那些人的想象，他们对民族生活应当是什么模样、一个民族应当为何而劳作，怀有高贵的构想——希望它有朝一日能够结实。本岛的人民身上悬着一道禁令：不得为军事目的实行征役。那么，我们有什么理由不为民事的目的实行征役呢？为什么爱尔兰的每一个青年，不该献出自己生命中的两年，与其他青年结成劳动的伙伴情谊，在熟练的指导下受雇于公益的大工程——营建公共建筑，美化我们的城市，开垦荒地，植树造林，以及种种其他可欲的事业？为军事目的向国家服役的原则，在每一个国家都得到承认，连说英语的诸民族最后也终于承认了它。不难看出，这一征役原则可以施用于无限高贵的诸目的——施用于一种美好文明的建构——并可使采纳它的国家不出半个世纪，便美丽如古代的阿提卡（Attica），庄严如古代的埃及。当其他民族取走青年的一部分生命以教习战争之术，爱尔兰这个国家——受着更高贵的灵感感召——为什么不能要求它的青年同样献出生命以奉国家：不为毁灭生命，而为保全生命？这项服役可以向所有人征取——不分尊卑，无论出身显赫还是寒微——只除开那些能援引理由求得豁免的人，一如国外的人民免服兵役所凭的那些理由。按今日的情形，国家若承办任何公共工程，其花销远远超过由私人企业承办的代价。人人都对国家、对市政当局抬价。劳力、土地与材料，一概按尽可能高的价钱索价；倘若公民身份与国家职能真有某种崇高的观念，公民们便会彼此约定：凡公益工程，凡协力增益国家尊严的事业，都应以对共同体最少的花费完成。没有民族的牺牲，便没有民族的自豪。正因为没有民族的自豪，我们诸现代文明在往昔诸城巨灵般的建筑与庄严的文明旁边，显得卑陋寒碜。从那些骄傲城市的废墟我们得知：走进古罗马、雅典（Athens）、底比斯（Thebes）、孟菲斯（Memphis）与巴比伦的人，行走在一派必然令精神为之昂扬的宏丽之中。而走进曼彻斯特（Manchester）、谢菲尔德（Sheffield）或利物浦（Liverpool），却是叫人感到灵魂上压着一副重担。在我们的工业文明里，没有对美的民族情感。

且让我们设想，爱尔兰经由产业民役（industrial conscription），每年有大约五万名青年置于一个国家工程部（department of national works）的调遣之下。那样能做成什么？首先，这意味着国内的每一个青年都将受过某种产业训练。技术教育（technical instruction）的工作，大可以依托这支产业大军进行。人们侈谈兵役所养成的纪律与服从之益。这一点，以及远不止这一点，都可以由一次劳役征召（labor conscription）予以确保。岛上的每一个人，都将在人生最需要养成它的时节养成工作的习惯——正是在这个年纪，太多的青年没有任何正经的营生。做父母的理应欢迎子女受这样的训练与纪律；而当公共服役期满，国家工程部颁发的品格与才智证书，应当为他们在本国寻常的产业生活中迅速就业开辟前景。对于投身其间的人，将有一种真正的、劳动中的伙伴情谊；而「劳动的尊严」（the dignity of labor）这个被写满了不知多少空话虚谈的字眼，也将在青年们为公共利益并肩劳作之处获得真实的意义——他们知道，任何一件落成的工程，都将增益这个国家的健康、美、尊严与繁荣。作为这份劳动的回报，国家应当为其产业大军提供衣食，教育他们，使他们熟习某一门营生，并使他们在服役期满之后更有本事投身私人企业。这样训练上两年，便可涤荡尽懒散、马虎与怠惰——这些毛病如此常见于那些在家从未受过任何严格管束、被父母的软弱弄得经不起生活之艰难营生的青年身上。

===Original===
The benefit to those undergoing such a training would of itself justify civil conscription; but when we come to think of the nation﻿—what might not be done by a State with a national labor army under its control? Public works might be undertaken at a cost greatly below that which would otherwise be incurred, and the estimates which now paralyze the State, when it considers this really needed service or that, would assume a different appearance, as it would be embracing in one enterprise technical education and the accomplishment of beneficial works. With such an army under skilled control the big cities could have playgrounds for the children of the cities; public gardens, baths, gymnasiums, recreation rooms, hospitals, and sanatoriums might be built; waste land reclaimed and afforested, and the roadsides might be planted with fruit trees. National schools, picture-galleries, public halls, libraries, and a thousand enterprises which now hang fire because at present labor for public service is the most expensive labor, all could be undertaken. If the State becomes very poor, as indeed it is certain to be, it may be forced into some such method of fulfilling its functions. Are we, with enormous burdens of debt, to hang up every useful public work because of the expense, and spend our lives in paying State debts while the body for whom we work is unable, on account of the expense, to do anything for us in return? If the State is to continue its functions we shall have to commandeer people for its service in times of peace as is done in times of war. There is hardly an argument which could be used to defend military conscription which could not be equaled with as powerful an argument for civil conscription. I am not at all sure that if the State in Ireland decided to utilize two years of every young man’s life for State purposes that we could not disband most of our expensive constabulary and make certain squads of our civil recruits responsible for the keeping of public law and order, leaving only the officers as permanent professionals, for of course there must be expert control of the conscripts. The postal service might also be carried on largely by conscripted civilians.

This may appear a fantastic programme, but I would like to see it argued out. It would create a real brotherhood in work, just as the army creates in its own way a brotherhood between men in the same regiments. The nation adopting civil conscription could clean itself up in a couple of generations, so that in respect of public services it would be incomparable. The alternative to this is to starve all public services, to make the State simply the tax-collector, to pay the interest on a huge debt, and so get it hated because it can do nothing except collect money to pay the interest on a colossal national debt. Obviously the State as an agency to bring about civilization cannot perform both services﻿—pay interest on huge public loans, and continue an expensive service. It must find out some way in which public services can be continued, and if possible improved, and the open way to that is civil conscription and the assertion of a claim to two or three years of the work of every citizen for civil purposes, just as it now asserts a claim on the services of citizens for the defense of the State. As national debts are more and more piled up, it has seemed to many that here must be an end to what was called social reform, that we were entering on a black era, and no dawn would show over Europe for another century. There is always a way out of troubles if people are imaginative enough and brotherly enough to conceive of it and bold enough to take action when they have found the way. The real danger for society is that it may become spiritless and hidebound and tamed, and have none of those high qualities necessary in face of peril, and the more people get accustomed to thinking of bold schemes the better. They will get over the first shock, and may be ready when the time comes to put them into action. When a country is poor like Ireland and yet is ambitious of greatness; when the aspect of its civilization is mean and when it yet aspires to beauty; when its people are living under unsanitary conditions and yet the longing is there to give health to all; when Ireland is like this, its public men and its citizens might do much worse than brood over the possibilities of industrial conscription, and of revising the character of the purposes for which nations have hitherto claimed service from their young citizens on behalf of the State. Debarred by a fate not altogether unkind from training every citizen in the arts of war Ireland might﻿—if the love of country and the desire for service are really so strong as we are told﻿—suddenly become eminent among the nations of the world by adopting a policy which in half a century would make our mean cities and our backward countryside the most beautiful in the modern world.

===Chinese===
单是受训者本人所得的益处，本身便足以证明民役的正当；可是，当我们进而想到整个民族——一个手中握有国民劳动大军的国家，还有什么做不成？公共工程便可以以远低于别种情形的花费兴办；如今，当国家掂量这项或那项确属必需的服务时，那些令国家瘫痪的概算也将换上一副面目，因为这一桩事业将把技术教育与公益工程的完成一并纳入怀抱。有这样一支大军置于熟练的统辖之下，大城市便能为城中的儿童辟出游戏场；公共花园、浴场、体育馆、娱乐室、医院与疗养院都可以建起来；荒地可以开垦、可以造林，路边可以栽上果树。国民学校、美术馆、公共会堂、图书馆，以及如今因公共服务的劳力索价最昂而悬置不动的一千种事业，统统都可以兴办。倘若国家变得十分贫穷——它必定会如此——它或许将被逼上某种这样的法子来履行其职能。难道我们背负着沉重的债务，就要因为花费而把每一项有用的公共工程都高高挂起，把我们的一生用来偿付国家债务，而我们为之效力的那个躯体，却因花费之故，无力为我们回报任何东西？国家若要继续履行其职能，我们就必须在和平时期也为它的服役征调人手，一如战时的做法。凡能用来为征兵制辩护的论据，几乎没有一条不能配上同样有力的一条为民役而发。我几乎毫不怀疑：倘若爱尔兰的国家决意把每个青年生命中的两年用于国家目的，我们尽可以解散我们那支靡费浩大的警察部队（constabulary）的大半，让我方民役新兵中的某些分队负起维护公共法律与秩序之责，只留下军官充任常任的职业人员——因为对被征者，当然必须施以专家的统辖。邮政也大可以主要由征召来的民役人员经办。

这在旁人看来或许是一份匪夷所思的纲领，但我愿意看到它被人认真辩难个明白。它将在劳作中创造出一种真正的兄弟情谊，正如军队以它自己的方式，在同一团的袍泽之间造就兄弟情谊。采行民役的民族，不出两三代人便能把自己洗刷一新，在公共服务一端无可比拟。舍此之外的另一条路，便是让一切公共服务饥馁至死，让国家沦为单纯的收税官，为一笔巨额债务偿付利息，于是落得被人憎恨——因为它除了敛钱偿付庞大国债的利息之外，一事无成。显然，国家作为缔造文明的一具机构，无法同时履行两桩差事——既偿付巨额公债的利息，又维持一项昂贵的服务。它必须找出某种法子，让公共服务得以延续，且如有可能还得以改善；而那条敞开的路，就是民役，就是主张每个公民都有两三年的劳作当为民事目的而征用——一如国家如今主张有权为保卫自身而征用公民的服役。随着国债越积越高，在许多人看来，所谓的社会改革（social reform）到此必有一个尽头，我们正在进入一个黑暗的年代，再过一个世纪也不会有黎明显现在欧洲上空。只要人们的想象力足以构想出路、手足之情足以共同怀抱它、寻得出路之后又足够果敢去行动，烦恼之中总归有一条出路。社会真正的危险，在于它可能变得毫无生气、僵化守旧、驯服顺从，丧尽面对危难时所必需的那些高贵品质；而人们越是习惯于思索大胆的方略，便越好。他们会挺过最初的震惊，待时机来临，或许已准备好将其付诸行动。当一个国家像爱尔兰这样贫穷，却怀着成就伟大的雄心；当它的文明面目卑陋，却又渴慕着美；当它的人民生活在不卫生的境况之中，而让人人得享健康的渴望又确然存在于彼——当爱尔兰是这般光景，它的公众人物与公民们，大可去沉思产业民役的种种可能，并重新审视各国迄今为代表国家向青年公民索取服役的目的所具有的性质——他们的作为，本很可能远逊于此。被一道并非全然无情的命运拦在门外、无缘教每一个公民研习战艺的爱尔兰——倘若对国家的爱与服役的热望，真如人们告诉我们的那般强烈——或许会因采行一项政策而骤然卓立于世界诸民族之林：不出半个世纪，这项政策便将使我们卑陋的城市与我们落后的乡野，成为现代世界最美之境。

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===

I have not in all this written anything about the relations of Ireland with other countries, or even with our neighbors, in whose political household we have lived for so many centuries in intimate hostility. I have considered this indeed, but did not wish, nor do I now wish, in anything I may write, to say one word which would add to that old hostility. Race hatred is the cheapest and basest of all national passions, and it is the nature of hatred, as it is the nature of love, to change us into the likeness of that which we contemplate. We grow nobly like what we adore, and ignobly like what we hate; and no people in Ireland became so anglicized in intellect and temperament, and even in the manner of expression, as those who hated our neighbors most. All hatreds long persisted in bring us to every baseness for which we hated others. The only laws which we cannot break with impunity are divine laws, and no law is more eternally sure in its workings than that which condemns us to be even as that we condemned. Hate is the high commander of so many armies that an inquiry into the origin of this passion is at least as needful as histories of other contemporary notorieties. Not emperors or parliaments alone raise armies, but this passion also. It will sustain nations in defeat. When everything seems lost this wild captain will appear and the scattered forces are reunited. They will be as oblivious of danger as if they were divinely inspired, but if they win their battle it is to become like the conquered foe. All great wars in history, all conquests, all national antagonisms, result in an exchange of characteristics. It is because I wish Ireland to be itself, to act from its own will and its own centre, that I deprecate hatred as a force in national life. It is always possible to win a cause without the aid of this base helper, who betrays us ever in the hour of victory.

When a man finds the feeling of hate for another rising vehemently in himself, he should take it as a warning that conscience is battling in his own being with that very thing he loathes. Nations hate other nations for the evil which is in themselves; but they are as little given to self-analysis as individuals, and while they are right to overcome evil, they should first try to understand the genesis of the passion in their own nature. If we understand this, many of the ironies of history will be intelligible. We will understand why it was that our countrymen in Ulster and our countrymen in the rest of Ireland, who have denounced each other so vehemently, should at last appear to have exchanged characteristics: why in the North, having passionately protested against physical force movements, no-rent manifestos, and contempt for Imperial Parliament, they should have come themselves at last to organize a physical force movement, should threaten to pay no taxes, and should refuse obedience to an Act of Parliament. We will understand also why it was their opponents came themselves to address to Ulster all the arguments and denunciations Ulster had addressed to them. I do not point this out with intent to annoy, but to illustrate by late history a law in national as well as human psychology. If this unpopular psychology I have explained was adopted everywhere as true, we would never hear expressions of hate. People would realize they were first revealing and then stabbing their own characters before the world.

===Chinese===

在以上所有这些文字里，我还不曾写过任何关于爱尔兰与其他国家关系的文字，甚至也不曾写过与我们邻邦的关系——在他们的政治家宅里，我们怀着亲密的敌意居住了这么多个世纪。这一点我确实考虑过，但我不愿——如今也不愿——在我可能写下的任何文字里，说出一句会加深那古老敌意的话。种族仇恨是一切民族激情中最廉价、最卑贱的一种；而仇恨的本性，恰如爱的本性，是把我们变成我们所凝望之物的模样。我们变得高尚地像我们所崇爱者，也变得卑贱地像我们所憎恨者；在爱尔兰，没有哪个群体像那些最恨我们邻邦的人那样，在智识与气质上、乃至在表达方式上变得如此英国化。凡长久怀持的仇恨，都会把我们引向每一种我们曾因之而恨他人的卑劣。唯有神圣法则，是违犯了而逃不脱惩罚的；而没有任何法则的运行，比那判令我们变得恰如我们所谴责之物的法则更永恒地确凿。仇恨麾下统率着为数如此众多的大军，以致对这种激情之源的探究，至少与为其他当代声名藉藉之辈所作的历史书写同样必要。招募军队的并不只是皇帝们与议会，这种激情也在招募。它能在败亡之中支撑起民族。当一切似乎都已丧失，这位狂野的统帅便会现身，涣散的力量便重新集结。他们对危险浑然忘怀，仿佛得了神启；然而倘若他们打赢了这一仗，结果却是变得与被征服的仇敌一般无二。历史上的一切大战、一切征服、一切民族对抗，其结局都是特性的交换。正因为我希望爱尔兰做它自己、依自己的意志、从自己的中心行事，我才反对把仇恨当作民族生活中的一种力量。不靠这个卑贱的帮手，一项事业也总是可能赢得——而这帮手，总在胜利的时刻背叛我们。

当一个人发觉对另一个人的憎恨之情在自己胸中激烈升起时，他应当把它看作一个警告：他的良心正在他自己的存在之中，与他自己所憎恶的那种东西搏斗。各民族之憎恨别的民族，恨的其实是自身之内的恶；但他们同个人一样不惯于自我剖析；他们克服恶固然是对的，却应当先设法理解这种激情在其自身天性中的起源。若懂得了这一点，历史上的许多反讽便都可以理解。我们便会明白，为什么我们在阿尔斯特（Ulster）的同胞与爱尔兰其他各地的同胞，彼此那样激切地互相谴责，到头来却显得交换了特性：为什么北方人在激烈抗议过武力运动（physical force movement）、抗租宣言与对帝国议会（Imperial Parliament）的蔑视之后，到头来竟自己组织起一场武力运动，竟威胁抗税不缴，竟拒绝服从一项议会法令。我们也会明白，为什么他们的对手到头来竟把阿尔斯特曾向他们发出的一切论据与谴责，反过来尽数发向阿尔斯特。我指出这些，并非有意招人不快，而是要借晚近的历史阐明一条民族的、亦即人类的心理学法则。倘若我阐明的这套不受欢迎的心理学处处被奉为真，我们就再也不会听到仇恨的言辞了。人们会意识到，他们这是在世界面前先袒露、继而刺伤自己的品格。

===Original===

Nations act towards other nations as their own citizens act towards each other. When slavery existed in a State, if that nation attacked another it was with intent to enslave. Where there is a fierce economic competition between citizen and citizen then in war with another nation, the object of the war is to destroy the trade of the enemy. If the citizens in any country could develop harmonious life among themselves they would manifest the friendliest feelings towards the people of other countries. We find that it is just among groups of people who aim at harmonious life, cooperators and socialists, that the strongest national impulses to international brotherhood arise; and wars of domination are brought about by the will of those who within a State are dominant over the fortunes of the rest. Ireland, a small country, can only maintain its national identity by moral and economic forces. Physically it must be overmastered by most other European nations. Moral forces are really more powerful than physical forces. One Christ changed the spiritual life of Europe; one Buddha affected more myriads in Asia.

===Chinese===

各民族怎样对待别的民族，恰如自己的公民怎样彼此相待。当一个国家尚存奴隶制时，这个民族若攻击他国，其用心便是奴役。哪里公民与公民之间存在激烈的经济竞争，那么一旦与别国交战，战争的目的便是摧毁敌国的贸易。倘若任何一国的公民能在彼此之间发展出和谐的生活，他们便会向别国民众表露最友好的情感。我们发现，恰恰是在那些以和谐生活为目标的人群——合作主义者与社会主义者——中间，生出了通往国际手足之情（international brotherhood）的最强烈的民族冲动；而征服战争，则是由那些在一国之内支配着其余众人命运者的意志铸成的。爱尔兰是个小国，唯有靠道德力量与经济力量，方能维系它的民族同一性。就物质力量而言，它必被多数其他欧洲国家所压倒。道德力量实在比物质力量更强大。一位基督改变了欧洲的精神生活；一位佛陀（Buddha）在亚洲感化的万千众生更多。

===Original===

The cooperative ideal of brotherhood in industry has helped to make stronger the ideal of the brotherhood of humanity, and no body of men in any of the countries in the great War of our time regarded it with more genuine sorrow than those who were already beginning to promote schemes for international cooperation. It must be mainly in movements inspired with the ideal of the brotherhood of man, that the spirit will be generated which, in the future, shall make the idea of war so detestable that statesmen will find it is impossible to think of that solution of their disputes as they would think now of resorting to private assassination of political opponents. The great tragedy of Europe was brought about, not by the German Emperor, nor by Sir Edward Grey, nor by the Czar, nor by any of the other chiefs ostensibly controlling foreign policy, but by the nations themselves. These men may have been agents, but their action would have been impossible if they did not realize that there was a vast body of national feeling behind them not opposed to war. Their citizens were in conflict with each other already, generating the moods which lead on to war. Emperors, foreign secretaries, ambassadors, cabinet ministers are not really powerful to move nations against their will. On the whole, they act with the will of the nations, which they understand. Let any one ruler try, for example, to change by edict the religion of his subjects, and a week would see him bereft of place and power. They could not do this, because the will of the nation would be against it. They resort to war and prepare for it because the will of the nation is with them, and this throws us back on the private citizens, who finally are individually and collectively responsible for the actions of the State. In the everlasting battle between good and evil, private soldiers are called upon to fight as well as the captains, and it is only through the intensive cultivation by individuals and races of the higher moral and intellectual qualities, until in intensity they outweigh the mood and passion of the rest, that war will finally become obsolete as the court of appeal. When there is a panic of fire in a crowded building men are suddenly tested as to character. Some will become frenzied madmen, fighting and trampling their way out. Others will act nobly, forgetting themselves. They have no time to think. What they are in their total make up as human beings, overbalanced either for good or evil, appears in an instant. Even so, some time in the heroic future, some nation in a crisis will be weighed and will act nobly rather than passionately, and will be prepared to risk national extinction rather than continue existence at the price of killing myriads of other human beings, and it will oppose moral and spiritual forces to material forces, and it will overcome the world by making gentleness its might, as all great spiritual teachers have done. It comes to this, we cannot overcome hatred by hatred or war by war, but by the opposites of these. Evil is not overcome by evil but by good; and any race like the Irish, eager for national life, ought to learn this truth﻿—that humanity will act towards their race as their race acts towards humanity. The noble and the base alike beget their kin. Empires, ere they disappear, see their own mirrored majesty arise in the looking-glass of time. Opposed to the pride and pomp of Egypt were the pride and pomp of Chaldea. Echoing the beauty of the Greek city state were many lovely cities made in their image. Carthage evoked Rome. The British Empire, by the natural balance and opposition of things, called into being another empire with a civilization of coal and steel, and with ambitions for colonies and for naval power, and with that image of itself it must wrestle for empire. The great armadas that throng the seas, the armed millions upon the earth betray the fear in the minds of races, nay, the inner spiritual certitude the soul has, that pride and lust of power must yet be humbled by their kind. They must at last meet their equals face to face, called to them as steel to magnet by some inner affinity. This is a law of life both for individuals and races, and, when this is realized, we know nothing will put an end to race conflicts except the equally determined and heroic development of the spiritual, moral, and intellectual forces which disdain to use the force and fury of material powers.

===Chinese===

产业中手足之情的合作理想，已助使人类手足之情的理想愈加坚固；而在我们时代的大战之中，各交战国没有任何一群人，比那些已着手推动国际合作（international cooperation）诸方案的人，更怀着真挚的悲恸看待这场战争。那种未来必将使战争观念变得如此可憎、使政治家们发觉再不可能像今日想到以私下暗杀政敌解决争端那样想到以战争解决争端的精神，主要必将在受人类手足之情之理想感发的诸运动中生成。欧洲的大悲剧并非由德意志皇帝、爱德华·格雷爵士（Sir Edward Grey）、沙皇，或任何其他表面上执掌外交政策的首领造成，而是由各民族自己造成。这些人或许只是代理人，但倘若他们不曾意识到身后有一大股并不反对战争的民族情绪，他们的行动便无从发生。他们的公民早已彼此冲突，正酝酿着引向战争的心境。皇帝们、外交大臣们、大使们、内阁大臣们，其实并没有违背民族意志而驱动民族的力量。大体而言，他们是循着民族意志行事的，而民族意志也是他们所了解的。譬如，任凭哪位统治者试着以一纸敕令改变其臣民的宗教，不出一个星期，他便会被剥夺职位与权力。他们做不到这样的事，因为民族的意志会反对它。他们诉诸战争并为之备战，是因为民族的意志与他们同在；而这就把我们推回到普通公民身上——国家的行动，最终正是由他们个别地且集体地承担责任的。在善与恶的永恒之战中，应召作战的不只是将帅，还有士卒；唯有靠个人与种族深耕更高的道德与智识品质，直到其强度压过其余众人的心境与激情，战争才会最终沦为陈迹，不再充作裁决争端的上诉法庭。当拥挤的楼宇之中发生火灾恐慌时，人的品格会骤然受试。有的人会变成狂乱的疯子，厮打着、踩踏着夺路而出。另一些人则会忘我地高贵行事。他们没有时间思考。他们作为人的整体构造——或倾于善，或倾于恶——在一瞬之间显露出来。即便如此，在那英雄的未来，总有一日，某个民族会在危机之中受称量，它将高贵地行事而非激情用事；它将甘冒民族覆灭之险，也不肯以屠戮万千他人为代价延续存在；它将以道德力量与精神力量对抗物质力量；它将以温柔为伟力而克服世界，一如一切伟大的精神导师之所为。归结起来便是：我们不能以仇恨克服仇恨，不能以战争克服战争，只能以它们的对立面克服它们。恶不能以恶克服，只能以善克服；任何像爱尔兰人这样渴求民族生命的种族，都应当学会这条真理——人类将怎样对待他们的种族，全看他们的种族怎样对待人类。高贵者与卑贱者同样各生其类。帝国在消逝之前，会在时间的镜中望见自己那镜像的威严升起。与埃及的骄矜华饰相对峙的，是迦勒底的骄矜华饰。与希腊城邦之美相呼应的，是许多依其模样造就的可爱城邦。迦太基（Carthage）唤出了罗马。大英帝国（the British Empire）凭事物自然的平衡与对立，唤起了另一个帝国——一个以煤与钢为文明、怀着攫取殖民地与海上强权之野心的帝国；而与它自己的这具镜像，它必得角力以争帝业。涌塞海洋的庞大舰队、布满大地的数百万武装，泄露出诸种族心中的恐惧——毋宁说，泄露出灵魂内在的精神确信：骄矜与权力之欲，终必被其同类所贬抑。它们终必与自己的匹敌者当面相遇，被某种内在的亲和力如磁石召引钢铁一般召到一处。这对个人与种族同为一条生命法则；而一旦认识到这一点，我们便知道：唯有同样坚决而英勇地发展那些不屑动用物质力量之强力与狂怒的精神、道德与智识力量，种族冲突方会终止。

===Original===

We may be assured that the divine law is not mocked, and it cannot be deceived. As men sow so do they reap. The anger we create will rend us; the love we give will return to us. Biologically, everything breeds true to its type: moods and thoughts just as much as birds and beasts and fishes. When I hear people raging against England or Germany or Russia I know that rage will beget rage, and go on begetting it, and so the whole devilish generation of passions will be continued. There are no nations to whom the entire and loyal allegiance of man’s spirit could be given. It can only go out to the ideal empires and nationalities in the womb of time, for whose coming we pray. Those countries of the future we must carve out of the humanity of today, and we can begin building them up within our present empires and nationalities just as we are building up the cooperative movement in a social order antagonistic to it. The people who are trying to create these new ideals in the world are outposts, sentinels, and frontiersmen thrown out before the armies of the intellectual and spiritual races yet to come into being. We can all enlist in these armies and be comrades to the pioneers. I hope many will enlist in Ireland. I would cry to our idealists to come out of this present-day Irish Babylon, so filled with sectarian, political, and race hatreds, and to work for the future. I believe profoundly, with the most extreme of Nationalists, in the future of Ireland, and in the vision of light seen by Bridget which she saw and confessed between hopes and tears to Patrick, and that this is the Isle of Destiny and the destiny will be glorious and not ignoble, and when our hour is come we will have something to give to the world, and we will be proud to give rather than to grasp. Throughout their history Irishmen have always wrought better for others than for themselves, and when they unite in Ireland to work for each other, they will direct into the right channel all that national capacity for devotion to causes for which they are famed. We ought not only to desire to be at peace with each other, but with the whole world, and this can only be brought about by the individual citizen at all times protesting against sectarian and national passions, and taking no part in them, coming out of such angry parties altogether, as the people of the Lord were called by the divine voice to come out of Babylon. It may seem a long way to set things right, but it is the swift way and the royal road, and there is no other; and nobody, no prophet crying before his time, will be listened to until the people are ready for him. The congregation must gather before the preacher can deliver what is in him to say. The economic brotherhood which I have put forward as an Irish ideal would, in its realization, make us at peace with ourselves, and if we are at peace with ourselves we will be at peace with our neighbors and all other nations, and will wish them the goodwill we have among ourselves, and will receive from them the same goodwill. I do not believe in legal and formal solutions of national antagonisms. While we generate animosities among ourselves we will always display them to other nations, and I prefer to search out how it is national hatreds are begotten, and to show how that cancer can be cut out of the body politic.

===Chinese===

我们可以确信：神圣法则是轻慢不得的，也是欺瞒不得的。人怎样撒种，便怎样收割。我们所制造的愤怒将撕裂我们；我们所给出的爱将回到我们身上。按生物学的通例，万物皆肖其类而繁衍：心境与思想，一如飞禽走兽与游鱼。每当我听见人们对英格兰、对德国或对俄罗斯怒骂不已，我便知道愤怒将生出愤怒，并且生生不息，于是那整族魔鬼谱系的激情便得以延续。世上没有任何一个国家，配承受人的精神完全而忠贞的效忠。这份效忠只能献给时间子宫中那些理想的帝国与民族——我们正为它们的降临而祈祷。那些未来的国家，我们必须从今日的人性中雕凿而出；而正如我们正在一种与之敌对的社会秩序中建设合作运动一样，我们也能在现存的帝国与民族之内开始缔造它们。世上那些努力创造这些新理想的人，是前哨、哨兵与拓边者，被派驻在尚待诞生的智识与精神诸种族的大军之前。我们人人都可以投身这些大军，做先驱者的同志。我希望在爱尔兰会有许多人从军。我要呼唤我们的理想主义者们走出这当今的爱尔兰巴比伦——它充塞着教派的、政治的与种族的仇恨——去为未来劳作。我与最极端的民族主义者们一样，深切地相信爱尔兰的未来，相信布里吉德（Bridget）所见的光之异象——她在希望与泪水之间看见并向帕特里克告白的那异象；我相信这就是命运之岛（the Isle of Destiny），而这命运将是光荣的，而非卑劣的；当我们的时辰到来，我们将有东西给予世界，我们将以给予为荣，而不以攫取为荣。纵观其历史，爱尔兰人为他人效力总胜过为自己效力；而当他们在爱尔兰联合起来、彼此效力之时，他们将把他们闻名于世的那种献身于事业的民族能力，尽数导入正道。我们不仅应当渴望彼此和平相处，也应当渴望与全世界和平相处；而唯有靠每一个公民时时抗议教派的与民族的激情、绝不参与其中、彻底走出这些愤怒的党派，这才可能实现——正如主的子民（the people of the Lord）曾受神圣之声的召唤走出巴比伦。这条正本清源之路看似漫长，却是迅捷之路、王者之路，且舍此别无他途；而任何人、任何在时代未至之先呼喊的先知，都不会被倾听，直到人民已为他准备停当。会众必须先聚集，布道者才能倾吐他胸中要说的话。我作为爱尔兰理想提出的那种经济上的手足之情，一旦实现，将使我们与自己和平相处；而我们若与自己和平相处，便将与我们的邻邦和一切别的国家和平相处，愿他们得享我们彼此之间的善意，也将从他们那里领受同样的善意。我不相信民族对立有什么法律与形式的解决办法。只要我们还在自己人中间制造敌意，我们就总会把这些敌意显示给别的国家；我宁愿去探明民族仇恨是如何生成的，去指明那毒瘤如何才能从政治体中割除。

## XX / 二十

===Original===
I beseech audience from the churches for these thoughts on our Irish polity, and would recall to them their early history, how when the fiery spirit of their Lord first manifested on earth, life, near to It, reflected It as in a glowing glass, and impulses of true living arose. Material possessions were held in common. There was no fierce talk of Thine and Mine. His ancient law counseled poverty to the spirit, lest the gates of Paradise should grow narrow before it like the eye of a needle. I believe the fading hold the heavens have over the world is due to the neglect of the economic basis of spiritual life. What profound spiritual life can there be when the social order almost forces men to battle with each other for the means of existence? I know well that no political mechanics, nothing which is an economic device only, will of themselves be able to affect the transfiguration of society and bring it under the dominion of the spirit. For that, a far higher quality of thought and action than is here indicated is necessary. The economist can provide the daily bread, but that bread of the coming day which Christ wished his followers to aspire to must come otherwise. That should be the labor of the poets, artists, musicians, and of the heroic and aristocratic characters who provide by their life an image to which life can be modeled. Therefore I beseech audience not only of the churches, but of the poets, writers, and thinkers of Ireland for their aid in this labor. They alone can create in wide commonalty the ideals which can dominate society. It is the work of the artist to create for us images of desirable life, to manifest to us the ideal humanity, and to prefigure that vaster entity which I have called the national being. I said in an earlier page that part of the failure of Ireland must be laid to the poets who had dropped out of the divine procession and sang a solitary song; to the writers who had turned from contemplating the great to the portrayal of the little in human nature. I know how difficult it is to constrain the spirit, and how futile it is to ask artists or poets to create what they are not inspired to create. But we can ask all men﻿—artists, poets, litterateurs, and scientists﻿—to be citizens, and if they realize imaginatively the spiritual conception of the State, we may assume that this imaginative realization of the State will influence the labors of the mind, and what is done will, consciously or unconsciously, have reference to that collective being which must dominate society more and more, which will dominate it as a tyranny if we fail in our labors, or liberate and make more majestical the spirit of man if we imagine rightly. All greatness is brought about by a conspiracy of the imagination and the will. Our literature certainly manifests beauty, but not greatness or majesty, for majesty only arises where there is an orchestration of humanity by some mighty conductor; and as a people we shall never manifest the highest qualities in literature or life until we are under the dominion of one, at least, of the great fundamental ideas which have been the inspiration of races. Our feebleness arises from our economic individualism. We continually neutralize each other’s efforts. Yet there is no less power in humanity today than there ever was. We see now clearly what untamed elemental fires lay underneath the seeming placidity of the world. There was a feeling in society that, just as the earth itself had settled down to be a habitable globe, and was forgetting its ancient ferocities of earthquake that opened up gulfs between land and land and rended sea from sea, so, too, humanity was losing those wilder energies we surmised in the cave-dweller or the hunters of mastodon, mammoth, and cave-tiger. But it was all a dream﻿—a dream, we suspect, about the earth as well as about humanity. While we indulged in these pleasing speculations on society, the scientists of our generation were placing beyond question or argument the doctrine of the indestructibility of energy and matter and we may be sure that while there is immortal life there must be immortal energies as its companions through time, and they will never be less powerful than they are today or were in the morning of the world. There will be no weakening of that mighty God-begotten brotherhood of elemental powers; and, while we cannot hope that by the wastage of time these powers will be feebler, we may hope that by an understanding of them we may get mastery over them. The wild elephant of the woods, with a greater strength than man’s, has yet been trained to be his servant, and that arcane power we call electricity, which, if it shoots out of its channel, shrivels up the body of man, is now our servant. So we may hope, too, that the elemental energies in humanity itself, which break out in wars and Armageddons, will come under control. We should not hope that man will ever be a less powerful being. To hope that would be to wish for his degradation. We should wish him to become ever more and more powerful by understanding himself, and by the unity of the spiritual faculties and the elemental energies in him into one harmonious whole. At present he is feeble because he is, to use the scriptural illustration, a house divided against itself.

Our feebleness is due to the conflict of powers in us and our conflict with each other. Get the two mightiest bulls in a herd, put them opposing each other in a narrow passage, and they, being of equal strength, will reduce each other to feebleness. Neither will make headway. Let them unite together in their charge, and what will oppose them? Men at conflict in their own hearts, opposing each other in the world, reduce themselves and each other to wretchedness. The race which could eliminate the factors which promote internal conflict in society and could organize human energies in harmony, would be powerful beyond our wildest dreams. Every now and then in world-history we come across instances of what organized humanity could accomplish. There are fragments of an architecture so majestic that they awe us as the high rocks of nature do, and they seem almost like portions of nature itself, and truly they are so, being portions of nature remade by man, who is also a nature energy of divine origin. Europe by its conflicts today is reducing itself to barbarism and powerlessness, and these conflicts arose out of the internal conflicts in society, for individuals and nations act outside themselves as they act inside themselves. The problem for Europe is to create a harmonious life, and it is the problem for us in Ireland, and we will have to work this out for ourselves. The creation of a harmonious life among a people must come from within. It can never come by the imposition of an external law imposed by another people: Never did master and slave work in true unison, no matter how benevolent the master or how yielding the slave, for there is in every man, no matter what his condition, a spark of divine life, and it will always be ready to stir him out of subjection, as the fires of earthquake lie below the cultivated plain. Man is a creature who has free will, and it is by self-devised and self-checked efforts he will attain his full human stature. So the problem of creating an organic life in Ireland, a harmony of our people, a union of their efforts for the common good and for the manifestation of whatever beauty, majesty, and spirituality is in us, must be one we ourselves must solve for ourselves.

===Chinese===
我恳请各教会垂听这些关于我们爱尔兰政体的思考，并愿向他们追述他们早年的历史：当他们的主的火热之灵初次显形于世时，贴近它的生命，如在一面炽亮的镜中映照出它，真正生命的冲动随之而起。那时，物质的财物归众人共有，没有关于「你的」「我的」的激烈争言。他古老的律法劝灵魂安于贫穷，免得天堂之门在它面前收窄，窄如针眼。我相信，诸天对尘世的掌控之所以日渐消退，缘于对精神生活之经济基础的忽视。当社会秩序几乎强迫人们为生存的手段而彼此相争时，还能有什么深厚的精神生活？我深知，任何政治机械、任何单纯的经济设计，都不能凭其自身促成社会的变容（transfiguration），把它带到精神的宰制之下。为此，需要远比我在此所指示的更为高贵的思想与行动的品质。经济学家能提供日用的食粮，但基督愿他的追随者企求的那来日的食粮，必须来自别处。那应当是诗人、艺术家与音乐家的劳作，也是那些英雄的、贵族式的品格的劳作——他们以自己的生命提供一个范像，生活可以据之以塑造。因此，我恳请垂听的不仅是各教会，还有爱尔兰的诗人、作家与思想家，恳请他们在这项劳作中施以援手。唯有他们能在广大的民众之中创造足以主宰社会的理想。艺术家的工作，正是为我们创造可欲生活的种种范像，向我们显明理想的人性，并预表那个我曾称之为国家存在的更宏大的实体。我在前文某页说过，爱尔兰的一部分失败，必须归咎于那些脱离了神圣的行列、唱着孤独之歌的诗人；归咎于那些从凝望伟大转而去刻画人性之渺小的作家。我知道约束精神何其困难，也知道要求艺术家或诗人去创造他们未受灵感催动的东西何其徒劳。但我们可以请求所有的人——艺术家、诗人、文人与科学家——做公民；倘若他们以想象领悟了国家的精神构想，我们便可以认定，这种对国家的想象性领悟将影响他们心智的劳作，他们所做的一切，无论自觉与否，都将关涉那个必将日益主宰社会的集体存在——倘若我们的劳作失败，它将如僭政一般主宰社会；倘若我们想象得正，它将解放人的精神，使之愈加庄严。一切伟大，皆由想象与意志的合谋造就。我们的文学固然显出美，却未显出伟大或庄严，因为庄严唯有人性受某位强有力的指挥者作管弦编配之处才会升起；而作为一个民族，除非我们至少处于一条曾启迪诸种族的伟大根本观念的统辖之下，我们便永远不能在文学或生活中显出最高的品质。我们的孱弱，源于我们的经济个人主义（economic individualism）。我们不断地相互抵消彼此的努力。然而今日人性中的力量，并不比往昔任何时候为少。如今我们清楚地看到，在世界的表面平静之下，躺着何等未经驯服的元素之火。当年社会上曾有一种感觉：正如大地本身已安定成一颗宜居的星球，正在忘却它古代地震的暴烈——那些在陆与陆之间撕开深渊、把海与海扯裂的地震——人性也在失去那些我们在穴居人身上、在乳齿象、猛犸与穴虎的猎手身上推想的更狂野的能量。但那全然是一场梦——一场我们疑心既关于人性、也关于大地的梦。当我们耽溺于这些关于社会的悦意玄想之时，我们这一代的科学家们，正把能量与物质不灭的学说置于不容置疑、不容置辩之地；而我们可以确信：只要还有不朽的生命，就必有不朽的能量与之偕行于时间之中，它们的威力绝不会逊于今日，也不会逊于世界的清晨。那元素力量之神生的宏大手足同盟，必不会有丝毫削弱；而且，虽然我们不能指望凭着时间的消磨使这些力量变弱，我们却可以指望凭着对它们的理解而获得对它们的驾驭。林中的野象，力气大过人类，却终究被驯成了人的仆役；那种我们称之为电的玄秘力量——它一旦窜出轨道，便会把人的身体灼得枯焦——如今也成了我们的仆役。所以我们也可以希望：人性自身之中那些爆发为战争与哈米吉多顿（Armageddon）之战的元素能量，终将归于驾驭。我们不应希望人成为力量更小的存在。那样希望，便是愿他堕落。我们应当愿他凭对自己的理解而变得日益强大，愿他体内的精神官能与元素能量统一为一个和谐的整体。今日他之所以孱弱，是因为——借用圣经的譬喻——他是一间自相纷争的房子。

我们的孱弱，源于我们内在诸力量的冲突，以及我们彼此之间的冲突。牵来牛群中两头最强壮的公牛，让它们在一条窄道里彼此顶立，它们力量相当，便会把彼此拖成孱弱，谁也前进不得。让它们合力发起冲锋，又有什么能阻挡它们？在内心里冲突、在世界上彼此作对的人们，使他们自己、也使彼此沦于悲惨。哪一个种族若能铲除助长社会内部冲突的种种因素，把人的能量组织得和谐一致，它的强大将远超我们最狂野的梦想。在世界历史中，我们时时会遇见一些例证，显示组织起来的人类所能成就的事功。有那么一些建筑的残段，庄严得令我们敬畏，一如自然界的高岩令我们敬畏；它们几乎就像是自然本身的一部分，而它们确实如此——它们是经人重造的自然的一部分，而人本身也是一种有着神圣来源的自然能量。欧洲正凭着它今日的种种冲突，把自己削减到野蛮与无力；而这些冲突正是从社会的内部冲突中生出的，因为个人与民族怎样在自身之内行事，便怎样在自身之外行事。欧洲的问题是要创造一种和谐的生活，这也是我们爱尔兰人的问题，而我们必须自己把它解答出来。一个民族之中和谐生活的创造，必须来自内部。它永远不能靠另一个民族从外部强加的法律来实现：主人与奴隶从未真正同心协作过，无论主人多么仁厚、奴隶多么顺从；因为每一个人，无论处于何种境况，都怀有一星神圣的生命之火，它随时准备把他从臣服中搅醒，一如地震之火躺在耕种的平原之下。人是有自由意志的造物，唯有凭着自我谋划、自我校验的努力，他才能达到他人性的完满身高。因此，在爱尔兰创造一种有机的生活、一种人民的和谐、一种为公共的善并为显明我们内在一切美、庄严与灵性而付出的努力的联合——这个问题，必须由我们自己为我们自己解决。

===Original===
To be indifferent to the possibilities of human life, to ignore the problem, is to turn our back on heaven, which fashioned the spirit of man in its image. If the spirit of man has likeness to Deity, it means that if it manifests itself fully in the world, the world too becomes a shadowy likeness of the heavens, and our civilizations will make a harmony with the diviner spheres. We give still a service of lip belief to the Scriptures, yet active faith we have not. But they are true, yesterday, today, and forever; and we have still the root of the matter in us, for when anyone utters out of profound conviction his faith, there are always multitudes ready to respond. What really prevents an organic unity in Ireland is the economic individualism of our lives. The science of economics deals with the efforts of men to mine out of nature the food, minerals, and materials necessary to preserve life. There is nothing more certain than that where men work alone or only with the aid of their families they are little higher than the animals. When they tend to unite civilization begins. Then arise the towers, the temples, the cities, the achievements of the architect and engineer. The earth is tapped of its arcane energies, the very air yields to us its mysterious powers. We control the etheric waves and send the message of our deeds across the ocean. Yet in the midst of these vast external manifestations of power, multitudes of men and women live in squalor, isolated in their labors, living in the slums of cities; and this, if we examine it, comes about because the organization of human energies into a harmonious unity is not complete. There is really no lack of food, clothing, building material, land. Nature has provided bountifully for more myriads than we are likely to see peopling the earth. But people compete with each other and undersell each other, and those who labor are mulcted of their due, and instead of turning to the earth﻿—the inexhaustible mother﻿—and working unitedly for the common weal, they continue that fierce competition and stultify each other’s efforts and reduce each other to wretchedness. Humanity is a house divided against itself. Those who feel this to be true must gather round any movement which gives a hope for the future, which indicates a policy by which the organic unity of society in Ireland might be attained, and our people work harmoniously to make beauty and health prevail in our civilization. What each gives up to society in the making of a civilization he gets back a thousandfold. Now, the cooperative movement alone of all movements in Ireland has aspired to make an economic solidarity in Ireland. Whatever the aims of other movements may be﻿—and many of them have high ideals and are necessary for the spiritual and intellectual development of our people﻿—there is none of them which has for aim the unity of economic life. They all leave untouched this problem﻿—how are we to organize society so that people will not be in conflict with each other, will not nullify each other’s efforts, but all will conspire together for unity, so that none shall be forgotten or oppressed or left out of our brotherhood? The policy I put forward is incomplete and imperfect, and it must necessarily be so, being mainly the work of one mind, and to complete it and perfect it there must be many minds and many workers fired by the ideal. But I have indicated in some completeness how the rural population could be cooperatively organized, federated together, and how the urban population could be organized and brought into a harmony of economic purpose with the folk of the country. Within the limits of object these suggestions amount to a policy for the nation.

If the tragic condition of the world leaves us unstirred, if we draw no lessons from it, if there is no fiery stirring of will in Ireland to make it a better place to live in, then indeed we may lose hope for our country. Let us remember the most scornful condemnation in Scripture was not given to the evil but to the indifferent: “Because thou art neither hot nor cold I will spew thee out of my mouth.” Let us not be the Laodiceans of Europe, listless and indifferent to human needs, swallowing our whisky and our porter, stupefying our souls, while our poor are sweated; letting the children of our cities die with more carelessness about life than the people of any other European country, with sectarian organization’s crawling in secrecy like poisonous serpents through the undergrowth of swamps and forests. The cooperative movement is at least open and ideal in its aims and objects. It is national and not sectional. It seeks the triumph of no section but the unity of our people, where unity alone is possible. Our intransigents and extremists of all parties are not hurt or wounded by their adhesion to the cooperative ideal. We may make up our minds that the stubborn Irish temperament will never be overcome, but it may be won, and the movement which invites all parties and creeds into its ranks and gives them the largest opportunities of working together and understanding each other, gives also the largest hope of the gradual melting of old bitterness into a common tolerance where what is best essentially wins; for all true triumphs are triumphs not of force, but the conquest by a superior beauty of what is less beautiful. We should aim at a society where people will be at harmony in their economic life, will readily listen to different opinions from their own, will not turn sour faces on those who do not think as they do, but will, by reason and sympathy, comprehend each other and come at last, through sympathy and affection, to a balancing of their diversities, as in that multitudinous diversity, which is the universe, powers and dominions and elements are balanced, and are guided harmoniously by the Shepherd of the Ages.

===Chinese===
对人类生命的种种可能漠不关心，对这个问题置之不理，就是背弃那按自己的形象塑造人之精神的天。人的精神若与神性相似，那就意味着：它若在世上充分显明自身，世界也将成为诸天的一个朦胧的肖像，我们的文明便将与更神圣的诸界构成一种和谐。我们至今仍以唇舌之信服事圣经，活泼的信心却是没有的。但圣经是真的——昨日、今日、直到永远；而我们里面也仍存着这道根，因为无论何人从深沉的确信中说出他的信仰，总有大群的人随时准备响应。真正阻碍爱尔兰有机统一的，是我们生活中的经济个人主义。经济学这门科学，处理的是人从自然中采掘维持生命所必需的食物、矿物与材料的种种努力。最确凿不过的是：凡人独自劳作、或仅得家人之助而劳作之处，人就比动物高不了多少。当他们趋于联合，文明便开始了。于是升起塔楼、神庙与城市，升起建筑师与工程师的种种成就。大地被凿取它玄秘的能量，连空气也向我们交付它奥秘的力量。我们驾驭着以太波（etheric waves），把我们功业的讯息送过大洋。然而，就在这些巨大的外在力量显现之中，成群的男男女女仍生活在污秽里，在劳作中彼此隔绝，栖身于城市的贫民窟；而细察之，这是由于人的能量向和谐统一体的组织尚未完成。食物、衣物、建材、土地，其实样样不缺。自然已为比我们可能见到的地上人口更多的万千众生，慷慨地备下了供给。但人们互相竞争、互相压价，劳作的人被侵夺应得之份；他们不去转向大地——那不竭的母亲——为公共福祉而协力同工，反而继续那惨烈的竞争，使彼此的努力归于落空，使彼此沦于悲惨。人类是一间自相纷争的房子。凡感到这是实情的人，都必须聚集到任何一个给出未来希望的运动周围——它指示出一条方针，循之或可达成爱尔兰社会的有机统一，使我们的人民和谐地劳作，让美与健康盛行于我们的文明。每个人在缔造文明时向社会交出的东西，他收回千倍。而在爱尔兰的一切运动之中，唯有合作运动曾立志要在爱尔兰造就一种经济上的团结。别的运动无论其目标为何——其中许多怀有崇高的理想，对我们人民的精神与智识发展是必要的——没有一个以经济生活的统一为目标。它们全都对这个问题未加触动：我们该怎样组织社会，使人们不再彼此冲突、不再互相抵消努力，而是全体为统一而协力同心，使没有一个被遗忘、被压迫、或被排除在我们的手足之情之外？我提出的方针是不完备、不完美的，它必然如此，因为它主要出自一个人的心智；要完成它、完善它，必须有许多的心智、许多为这理想所点燃的劳动者。但我已经相当完整地指明：乡村人口可以怎样以合作的方式组织起来、联合成会；城市人口可以怎样组织起来，与乡野的民众在经济目的上达成和谐。在本书宗旨的限度之内，这些建言合起来便构成一项民族的政策。

倘若世界的悲惨境况不能搅动我们，倘若我们从中汲取不到任何教训，倘若爱尔兰没有一股火热的意志之激荡要把她变成一个更宜于生活的所在，那么我们确实可以对自己的国家失去希望了。让我们记住：圣经中最轻蔑的谴责，不是加给恶人的，而是加给冷漠者的——「因你既不冷也不热，我必从我口中把你吐出去」。让我们不要做欧洲的老底嘉人（Laodiceans）：对人的需要倦怠冷漠，灌着我们的威士忌与黑啤酒，麻痹自己的灵魂，而与此同时我们的穷人正受着血汗盘剥；任我们城市的孩童死去，其对生命的漠然甚于任何其他欧洲国家的人民；任凭教派组织像毒蛇一般，隐秘地爬行于沼泽与森林的下层林莽之间。合作运动至少在宗旨与目标上是坦荡而理想的。它是全民族的，而非一派一系的。它谋求的不是任何一派的得胜，而是我们人民的统一——因为唯有统一在此才有可能。我们各党的不妥协者与极端主义者，并不会因皈依合作理想而受损伤。我们可以认定：爱尔兰倔强的气质永不会被克服，却可以被赢取；而这场把各党各派、各种信仰邀入自己的行列、给他们以同工与相知的最大机会的运动，也就给出了最大的希望——让古老的怨毒渐渐熔化为一种共同的宽容，在其中，本质最好的东西终将获胜；因为一切真正的胜利，都不是强力的胜利，而是更卓异的美对不那么美者的征服。我们应当以求这样一种社会为目标：人们在其经济生活中彼此和谐，乐于倾听不同于自己的意见，不对想法与自己不同的人板起阴沉的脸，而是凭理性与同情彼此领会，并最终通过同情与钟爱，达到对彼此差异的一种平衡——正如在那万千殊异之总体、即宇宙之中，诸大能、诸统权与诸元素各得其平衡，和谐地受着历代之牧者（the Shepherd of the Ages）的引导。
